COMMAND:
python3 << 'PY'
# Problem found: "Admin ने restore किया" is inside dollar-quoted string $q$...$q$
# But the check still finds non-ASCII INSIDE the $q$...$q$ block — that's CORRECT behavior
# Dollar-quoted strings in PostgreSQL DO support unicode natively! No need to escape them.
# The REAL problem is: when we json.dumps with ensure_ascii=True, the $q$ content
# with unicode becomes \uXXXX in JSON → postgres receives literal \uXXXX text, not unicode
# 
# Solution: Send JSON with ensure_ascii=FALSE — the issue was earlier with ::uuid parsing
# Let's test WHY the original ensure_ascii=False failed — it was "trailing junk after numeric literal"
# That error is about UUID parsing, NOT unicode! Let me re-read the error:
# "trailing junk after numeric literal at or near '8604b526'"
# 8604b526 is part of a UUID '8604b526-23e5-44ab-...'
# This error means the SQL itself has a syntax problem unrelated to unicode!

# Let's look at profiles INSERT line 22 (index 21) — the one BEFORE the failing line 23
import re
with open('/tmp/public_ordered.sql', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:
    content = f.read()

blocks = {}; cur = None
for line in content.split('\n'):
    if re.match(r'^-- \w', line) and '::' not in line and 'End of' not in line:
        cur = line[3:].strip(); blocks[cur] = []
    elif cur:
        blocks[cur].append(line)

profile_inserts = [l.strip() for l in blocks.get('profiles',[]) if l.strip().startswith('INSERT')]
print(f"Total profiles inserts: {len(profile_inserts)}")

# Check line 22 (index 21) for syntax issues
line22 = profile_inserts[21]
print(f"\nLine 22 length: {len(line22)}")
print(f"Ends with: {repr(line22[-50:])}")
# Does it end with semicolon?
print(f"Ends with semicolon: {line22.rstrip().endswith(';')}")
# Check for any special chars that could break SQL
special = [(i,c,hex(ord(c))) for i,c in enumerate(line22) if c in (';','\n','\r','\x00')]
print(f"Special chars: {special[:10]}")
# Print full line 22
print(f"\nFULL LINE 22:\n{line22}")

# ROOT CAUSE FOUND: Line 22 is INCOMPLETE — it was split across multiple lines!

# The export SQL has multiline text values (bio contains newline characters).

for line in profiles_section.splitlines():
    if line.startswith('INSERT INTO'):
        if current_stmt:
            inserts_complete.append('\n'.join(current_stmt))
        current_stmt = [line]
    elif current_stmt and line.strip():
        current_stmt.append(line)
    elif current_stmt and not line.strip() and current_stmt and '\n'.join(current_stmt).rstrip().endswith(';'):
        inserts_complete.append('\n'.join(current_stmt))
        current_stmt = []

if current_stmt:
    inserts_complete.append('\n'.join(current_stmt))

print(f"\nComplete INSERT statements found: {len(inserts_complete)}")

    print(f"\n--- Statement {i+1} ({len(stmt)} chars, {stmt.count(chr(10))+1} lines) ---")

import requests, json, re

PAT = "sbp_17a2f53bacd3a600904da2ce484de6fb830c32ea"
NEW_PROJECT = "jfizzduvmzavtqwzqacy"
URL = f"https://api.supabase.com/v1/projects/{NEW_PROJECT}/database/query"
HEADERS = {"Authorization": f"Bearer {PAT}", "Content-Type": "application/json"}

def parse_table_inserts(content, table_marker):
    """Extract complete INSERT statements for a table, handling multiline values."""
    start = content.find(f'-- Table: {table_marker}')
    if start == -1:
        return []
    # Find next table marker
    next_table = re.search(r'-- Table: ', content[start+10:])
    end = start + 10 + next_table.start() if next_table else len(content)
    section = content[start:end]
    
    stmts = []
    current = []
    for line in section.splitlines():
        if line.startswith('INSERT INTO'):
            if current:
                stmts.append(' '.join(current))
            current = [line]
        elif current:
            stripped = line.strip()
            if stripped:
                current.append(stripped)
            elif current and ' '.join(current).rstrip().endswith(';'):
                stmts.append(' '.join(current))
                current = []
    if current:
        stmts.append(' '.join(current))
    return stmts

def send_inserts(name, stmts):
    if not stmts:
        print(f"  ⏭️  {name}: no data"); return True
    # Send as proper UTF-8 JSON — use ensure_ascii=False
    # The UUID "trailing junk" was from /tmp/public_ordered.sql (which had split lines)
    # Original data_export.sql has proper single-line INSERTs
    sql = '\n'.join(stmts)
    resp = requests.post(URL, headers=HEADERS,
        data=json.dumps({"query": sql}, ensure_ascii=False).encode('utf-8'),
        timeout=60)
    if resp.status_code in (200, 201):
        print(f"  ✅ {name} ({len(stmts)} rows)"); return True
    else:
        print(f"  ❌ {name}: {resp.text[:300]}"); return False

with open('/workspace/app-cjmldrzgvw1t/tasks/data_export.sql', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:
    content = f.read()

# Import in FK order using original file
ORDER = [
    'profiles', 'posts', 'stories', 'reels',
    'follows', 'likes', 'comments', 'reel_comments', 'reel_likes', 'reel_views',
    'saved_posts', 'messages', 'notifications',
    'story_likes', 'story_views', 'online_status', 'blocks',
    'verification_requests', 'reports', 'appeals', 'problem_reports',
    'broadcast_notifications', 'activity_logs',
]

results = {}
for t in ORDER:
    stmts = parse_table_inserts(content, f'public.{t}')
    ok = send_inserts(t, stmts)
    results[t] = ok

ok_count = sum(1 for v in results.values() if v)
print(f"\n=== RESULT: {ok_count}/{len(results)} tables imported ===")
for t, ok in results.items():
    if not ok:
        print(f"  FAILED: {t}")
